Religion. 
— ſeem to us to have any fix' d Notion of Him; 
it is true, we met with ſome on our Way, who 
as far as we could judge, believ'd, there was 
ſome Superior Being, which was- above all 
Things, and this they teſtify'd by lifting up 
their Hands and Eyes to Heaven, yet without 
any Manner of Concern, as believing that the 
 Fdaid exalted Being does not regard at all, what is 
done here below. However none of them 
| having any Places of Worſhip, Ceremonies, 
or Prayers, to denote the divine Homage, it 
may be ſaid of them all, that they have no Re- | 
Iungion, at leaſt thoſe that we ſaw. 
Ka but whether they have any Regard to a realor | 
| pretended Superior Being, or whether they are 
only popular, and proceeding from Cuſtom, is

The City of Mexico, Capital of the Country Z 
and the Reſidence of the Vice-roy, is about 
ceighty Leagues diſtant from Veracruz, to the 
the Way to it very bad and ii

| nltivated by the Inhabitants. They ſow but 
little of our Wheat, and are ſatisfied with Iudian 


Trees and Fruits are the ſame as in other hot 
Countries. About the Town of Feracraz, there 


among which grows an extraordinary Plant; 
for tho? it has but a ſmall Stem, it ſhoots out 


Finger, which grow out, one at the End of ano- 
ther, in the Shape of a Racket, and the Plant 


itſelf is ſo call d. From thoſe Leaves there 
grows out a Sort of red Figs, very juicy, with 


Seeds like thoſe of the Pomgranate; the juice 


is of a Violet Colour, but unſavoury. There 
is a Sort of Flies that cleave to it znd 
are fo fond of the Taſte of the Fruit, that 

they burſt and drop down dead. They are 


carefully gather'd and dry'd, and are the Scar- 


let Dye, call'd Cochinilla, which is brought |
